## A5.8 Increased reliance of health and well-being, and food and water security

The Project will promote multi-stakeholder dialogue, bringing together actors from sectors such as public health, agriculture, education, and climate change adaptation to discuss the linkages between climate change and human health. This will include understanding of the impacts of climate change on health and food security, and how these can be addressed through improved health systems and food security policies. The Project will also support the development of early warning systems for climate-related health risks, which will help to improve preparedness and response to future climate-related health emergencies.

## A3.8 Strengthened adaptive capacity and reduced exposure to climate risks

The Project will establish a people-centred, project-based multi-hazard early warning system (MHEWS), which will enhance the resilience of communities to climate change impacts.

### A3.8.1 Improved access to climate information services

The Project will work with meteorological agencies to improve the availability and use of climate information services for agricultural planning and commercial farming on sustainable agriculture. It will provide training and awareness workshops to build capacity for weather and climate services. The Project will also enhance the capacity of farmers, especially women, to adapt to climate change through improved knowledge and understanding of disaster risk to increase their resilience.

### A3.8.2 Strengthened awareness of climate threats and risk-reduction processes

The Project will strengthen the capacity of communities to understand and respond to climate risks and opportunities by enhancing their ability to access and use climate information services. The Project will work with communities and partners to develop local understanding of climate processes and risk-reduction measures, including early warning systems.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Introduction to the evidence-based planning and decision-making for climate resilience

Building upon the success of the GCF-funded UNDP project FP108, this proposal aims to enhance the capacity of the Government of the Republic of Fiji (GoFR) in evidence-based planning and decision-making for climate-resilient infrastructure. This will support a sustainable approach to the Government's sectors and communities affected by climate change and disaster risk management.

### Objectives

- **Objective 1:** To strengthen the capacity of the GoFR to enhance their resilience to climate change.
- **Objective 2:** To build the capacity of vulnerable and at-risk climate adaptation projects with the traditional knowledge of local communities, particularly women, youth, and persons with disabilities. This will be achieved through a wide network, including and involving management activities, environmental protection, and sustainable livelihoods.

### Expected Outcomes

- **Outcome 1:** Enhanced data verification for critical infrastructure impacted by climate change.
- **Outcome 2:** Improved planning, design, construction, and management frameworks to significantly reduce the impact of natural disasters on communities.
- **Outcome 3:** Enhanced capacity of local governments in Fiji to manage disaster risk. This will be facilitated through the development of local Community Disaster Management Plans (CDMPs) and the integration of climate change adaptation into national policies.

### Project Description

As described in section D.1, the project will continue the GCF-funded UNDP project FP108, made possible by the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The project aims to build on the success of FP108 and enhance the capacity of the GoFR to integrate climate change adaptation into national policies and plans. This will be achieved through a combination of technical assistance, policy dialogue, and capacity building.

The project will also seek to leverage the UNDP's sub-national actors and regional partnerships to support the implementation of the National Adaptation Plan (NAP) process. The project will contribute to the achievement of the NAP by providing technical assistance to the GoFR in the development of a comprehensive national adaptation plan, including the integration of climate change adaptation into national policies.

### Project Management

The project will be implemented through a combination of technical assistance and capacity building activities. This will include the provision of training, workshops, and other support to the GoFR in the development of the NAP process. The project will also seek to leverage the UNDP's sub-national actors and regional partnerships to support the implementation of the NAP.

### Project Funding

The project is funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) through the UNDP. The total funding for the project is USD 10 million, with the GoFR contributing USD 2 million in co-financing. The project will be implemented over a period of five years, from 2023 to 2028.

### Project Implementation

The project will be implemented by the UNDP in collaboration with the GoFR and other relevant stakeholders. The project will be implemented through a combination of technical assistance and capacity building activities. This will include the provision of training, workshops, and other support to the GoFR in the development of the NAP process.

### Project Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated by the UNDP and the GoFR. The monitoring and evaluation framework will be developed in consultation with the GoFR and other relevant stakeholders. The monitoring and evaluation framework will include indicators for tracking progress towards the achievement of the project's objectives.

# Environmental co-benefits

The long-term environmental co-benefits of the Project are expected to be significant. The increased use of renewable energy will reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance climate stability. The Project will also contribute to improved air quality through climate-cool-based cogeneration to conservation and arresting biodiversity loss.

The Project will avoid greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions by replacing fossil fuel-based electricity generation with renewable energy sources. This will help in reducing the carbon footprint of the region and contribute towards mitigating climate change. The Project will also reduce emissions from fossil fuels, which are a significant source of air pollution. This will improve air quality and reduce health risks associated with exposure to pollutants.

The Project will establish a high-reliability grid that will ensure the efficient use of renewable energy resources. This will help in reducing the need for backup power plants and enhance the overall efficiency of the electricity system. The Project will also promote the development of new technologies and practices that can be used to further reduce emissions from the electricity sector.

The Project will contribute to the conservation of biodiversity by protecting natural habitats and promoting sustainable land use practices. This will help in preserving the ecological integrity of the region and supporting the conservation of endangered species.

The Project will also contribute to the reduction of water consumption by using renewable energy sources that require less water than fossil fuel-based power plants. This will help in conserving water resources and reducing the impact of electricity generation on water availability.

At the global scale, the enhanced deployment of essential surface-based observation data will enhance global climate models and improve predictions of future climate change. The Project will also contribute to the development of new technologies and practices that can be used to further reduce emissions from the electricity sector.

Moreover, the Project brings enhancements directly to communities, enhancing local economic development, quality of life, and health. Quality of life improvements are particularly important in regions where environmental hazards are high. The Project achieves a win-win by considering the level to avoid multifaceted risks that affect human health and well-being. The Project also provides an opportunity for local communities to work together to address these issues.

Social co-benefits including health impacts

The Project will have significant social benefits, particularly with regard to positive impacts on health and population well-being. Climate and health are inherently linked. There is an increasing body of evidence that demonstrates the impact of climate change on human health. The Project also provides an opportunity to improve decision-making for public health policies. There is also an urgent need to strengthen the links between the environmental, social, and health communities to understand and reduce the health risks of climate change.

### Executive Summary

The Project will benefit women, gender, and economic models. The contribution of these evaluations, provided that they are conducted in a participatory manner, will be used to improve the Project’s Co-benefit Analysis (Version 3). This analysis method was deemed most suitable for co-benefits assessment with potential interventions performed in Tanzania and the Pacific Islands.

The Project will facilitate gender-responsive development in Temeke District through the mainstreaming of gender considerations throughout design, implementation, and monitoring. The project aims to empower women and girls as leaders and decision-makers in the community and to address the gender inequalities that are not reflected. This includes ensuring the inclusion of women in leadership positions.

The Project will aim to:

1. Increase gender awareness and increase understanding at national, sectoral, and community levels of gender equality issues.
2. Address the concerns, vulnerabilities, of both, women and men through meaningful stakeholder engagement and the delivery of climate solutions and early warning services tailored to specific needs.
3. Promote the empowerment of women through equal representation and participation in planning, implementation, and monitoring.

A summary is provided below on how the Project will be implemented and what is in the Project provided in section 0.2.

### Impact of COVID-19 on the Global Observing System - a potential co-benefit of the Project

The impact of COVID-19 on the global observing system has been significant. The pandemic has led to the closure of many observation sites, which has resulted in a reduction in data collection and analysis. This has had a direct impact on the ability of countries to monitor and respond to climate change.

What a significant portion of the global observing system is either very old or fully automated, some countries have been able to maintain their systems during the pandemic. However, many countries have seen a reduction in data collection due to the pandemic. This has had a direct impact on the ability of countries to monitor and respond to climate change.

The Project will aim to address this issue by providing support for the development of new observation sites and the maintenance of existing ones. The Project will also aim to provide training and capacity building for those involved in the collection and analysis of data.

### Impact of COVID-19 on lives, livelihoods, and exists - a potential co-benefit of implementing the Project

The impact of COVID-19 on lives, livelihoods, and exists has been significant. The pandemic has led to the closure of many businesses and the loss of jobs for millions of people around the world. This has had a direct impact on the ability of countries to respond to climate change.

The Project will aim to address this issue by providing support for the development of new observation sites and the maintenance of existing ones. The Project will also aim to provide training and capacity building for those involved in the collection and analysis of data.

## Key Findings

- A 2019 study on investment in Africa indicates that the average cost of capital is USD 30 million, with an average return on equity (ROE) of 15%.
- A 2018 study on investment in Asia indicates that the average cost of capital is USD 4.3 billion, with an average ROE of 27% per year.

## Additional Studies

- A 2019 study on investment in Africa, Kenya and Somalia indicates that early response to drought, floods and other natural disasters can reduce economic losses by up to USD 5 million.
- A 2018 study on investment in Asia, India and Pakistan indicates that the average cost of capital is USD 4.3 billion, with an average ROE of 27% per year.

The Project includes specific measures for the protection of biodiversity, including the conservation of natural habitats and species, and the restoration of degraded ecosystems. The Project will also contribute to the development of sustainable agriculture systems and practices that enhance food security and nutrition, while promoting climate resilience and adaptation. The Project will support the implementation of integrated water resource management and land use planning, as well as the promotion of sustainable energy and transport systems.

The Project will be implemented in two phases: a preparatory phase (2019-2021) and an implementation phase (2022-2030). The Project will be financed through a combination of grants from the Green Climate Fund and other sources. The total cost of the Project is estimated at USD 500 million, with the Green Climate Fund providing up to USD 300 million in grant funding.

The Government of Timor-Leste has highlighted the critical importance of services relating to climate change, early warning systems for climate-related hazards, and disaster risk reduction (DRR) in major national policy documents. The Government has also emphasized the need for a comprehensive approach to climate change adaptation that integrates DRR into all sectors of society.

Dialogue on this Project began in 2015 when Timor-Leste expressed interest in the World Meteorological Organization's (WMO) Integrated Global Greenhouse Gas Information System (IG3IS). The Government has also been engaged with the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) Secretariat and the WMO to develop a National Adaptation Plan (NAP).

The Secretary of State for the Environment (SOE) in Timor-Leste acts as the lead Cultural Entity (LEI) for the Project. The SOE is responsible for the overall implementation of the Project and will build in country capacity to lead on future climate change adaptation efforts.

The Project will be implemented by the Ministry of Agriculture and Fisheries (MAF), which has been engaged at the national level on their work related to climate change and disaster risk reduction. The MAF will also be responsible for the implementation of the Project, with support from the SOE and other relevant ministries.

The Project will be implemented in close collaboration with the Government of Timor-Leste, the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP), and other partners. The Project will be implemented in accordance with the UNDP's policies and procedures.

#### Engagement with civil society organisations

The Government of Timor-Leste is requesting a GCF grant to enable the efficient and effective delivery of the project. The project grants are conditional upon the availability of co-financing as follows: the Government of Timor-Leste will provide USD 500,000; the Asian Development Bank (ADB) will provide USD 1 million; and the World Bank will provide USD 2 million.

The Cost-Benefit Analysis (CBA) has determined and assessed the costs and benefits of the proposed project. The CBA has been conducted in accordance with the GCF's Policy on Environmental and Social Safeguards (ESS), the GCF's Policy on Gender Equality, and the GCF's Policy on Indigenous Peoples' Issues.

The CBA has identified a range of benefits from the project, including increased income for farmers, improved health outcomes, reduced disaster risk, and enhanced community resilience. The CBA has also identified a number of costs associated with the project, including the cost of implementing the project, the cost of training local communities in disaster risk management, and the cost of providing technical assistance to the Government of Timor-Leste.

The CBA has determined that the benefits of the project outweigh the costs by a factor of 2:1. The CBA has also identified a number of risks associated with the project, including the risk of natural disasters, the risk of political instability, and the risk of financial constraints. The CBA has recommended that the Government of Timor-Leste implement a series of measures to mitigate these risks, including the establishment of a disaster risk management fund, the provision of technical assistance to local communities, and the implementation of a monitoring and evaluation system.

The CBA has also identified a number of opportunities for enhancing the benefits of the project, including the potential for increased investment in disaster risk management, the potential for enhanced coordination between different stakeholders, and the potential for enhanced integration of gender equality and social inclusion considerations into the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL

## Overview

Quantitative analysis, such as social and environmental benefits derived from the Project, is essential to demonstrate that the Project is sustainable and contributes to climate change mitigation. Most of the benefits are intangible and difficult to quantify in monetary terms. As a result, the CBA (Annex 2) provides a qualitative assessment for the Project, which is essential to present a sensible overview of the Project's benefits.

### Benefits Calculated

The benefits calculated in the CBA take the form of avoided economic damages and losses. Without the Project, the baseline scenario would be business as usual, with no additional measures taken to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The benefits are estimated by comparing the baseline scenario with the Project scenario. The benefits include:

- **Health benefits**: Avoidance of premature mortality and morbidity due to air pollution.
- **Economic benefits**: Avoided damages from climate change impacts on agriculture, forestry, fisheries, tourism, and other sectors.
- **Environmental benefits**: Reduced emissions of pollutants such as particulate matter (PM), sulfur dioxide (SO2), nitrogen oxides (NOx), and carbon monoxide (CO).

### Project Benefits

The Project contributes to the following Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs):

1. **SDG 3 - Good Health and Well-being**
   - Improved air quality, leading to better health outcomes.
   - Reduced exposure to pollutants.

2. **SDG 7 - Affordable and Clean Energy**
   - Increased use of renewable energy sources.
   - Enhanced energy efficiency.

We will also ensure that all infrastructure, systems and processes are compliant with the WMO Global Basic Observing Network (GBWN) standards and guidelines.

The GCF Readiness Programme will further enhance its direct contribution to the national processes and strengthening of existing national mechanisms. For example, the National Disaster Risk Management Policy Framework of the Philippines, developed through the GCF Readiness Programme, demonstrates the commitment, made the policy a multi-sector responsibility, and allocated the necessary resources for implementation.

Solidarity (SOLID), acts as a lead organisation for GCF in the country and coordinates with all line-ministries. The GCF National Designated Authority (NDA) is also part of SOLID. The NDA will enhance ownership through proper engagement of all relevant stakeholders, including local governments, to ensure that the decision-making becomes less of an elite- and more inclusive process.

Further details can be found in the Economic Analysis (Annex 3).

## Submissions

### A. Project Title
**Title:** Strengthening Climate Resilience and Sustainable Livelihoods in Vulnerable Communities

### B. Project Description
**Description:** The project aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities by implementing climate-smart agriculture practices, improving water management systems, and promoting sustainable livelihoods.

### C. Project Objectives
1. Increase access to climate-resilient agricultural technologies.
2. Improve water management systems for efficient use of water resources.
3. Promote sustainable livelihoods through capacity building and microfinance initiatives.

## The Proposal

### A. Executive Summary
**Summary:** This proposal outlines the project's objectives, activities, and expected outcomes. It focuses on enhancing climate resilience in vulnerable communities by implementing sustainable practices.

### B. Project Details
**Project Title:** Strengthening Climate Resilience and Sustainable Livelihoods in Vulnerable Communities

**Location:** [Specific location details]

**Duration:** [Start and end dates of the project]

**Objective:** To enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate change through sustainable practices, improved water management systems, and livelihood support.

### C. Project Activities
1. **Climate-Smart Agriculture:**
   - Training on climate-resilient agricultural practices.
   - Distribution of climate-smart seeds and tools.
   - Establishment of demonstration farms.

2. **Water Management Systems:**
   - Construction of rainwater harvesting systems.
   - Rehabilitation of existing water infrastructure.
   - Training on efficient water use and management.

3. **Livelihood Support:**
   - Microfinance initiatives for sustainable livelihoods.
   - Capacity building through training and workshops.
   - Establishment of community-based enterprises.

## The GCF will be supported by a Technical Assistance (TA) component to:

- Improve climate knowledge and understanding of climate change issues.
- Enhance capacity for planning, implementing, monitoring, reporting, and evaluating the implementation of projects.
- Develop business plans and project proposals.
- Provide technical assistance in developing and implementing the projects.

## Objectives

To improve the capacity of the National Designated Authority (NDA) to:

1. **National Climate Data:**
   - Collect, analyze, and disseminate climate data.
   - Establish a national climate information system.
   - Develop and implement a national climate change adaptation strategy.
   - Integrate climate risk management into sectoral planning.

2. **Technical Support and Capacity Building:**
   - Provide technical support to the NDA for climate change management, governance, and policy development.
   - Build capacity of key stakeholders in climate change adaptation and mitigation.
   - Develop and implement a national climate change adaptation strategy.
   - Integrate climate risk management into sectoral planning.

3. **Project Preparation:**
   - Support the preparation of projects to be submitted for GCF funding.
   - Provide technical assistance to develop project proposals.
   - Conduct feasibility studies, environmental impact assessments, and other necessary preparatory activities.
   - Develop a pipeline of projects ready for GCF funding.

4. **Capacity Building:**
   - Build capacity of key stakeholders in climate change adaptation and mitigation.
   - Develop and implement a national climate change adaptation strategy.
   - Integrate climate risk management into sectoral planning.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V2.1 | PAGE 84 OF 119

## 2.1 Enhance National Meteorological and Hydrological Services (NMHS) capabilities for climate services and early warning monitoring

The Activity will enhance the capacity of NMHS to deliver climate information and services through improved access to climate data, tools, and technologies. Enhanced Application Program Interfaces (APIs), web-based visualization tools, and mobile applications will enable NMHS to provide timely and accurate climate information to users in a user-friendly format. The Activity will also support the development of a National Climate Services Platform that will integrate climate data from various sources and provide a centralized platform for accessing climate information and services.

### 2.1.1 Expand and upgrade observation networks

- Strengthen existing observation networks
- Implement advanced monitoring technologies to improve data collection
- Upgrade NMHS data centers to ensure reliable storage and processing of climate data
- Enhance data dissemination capabilities through improved web-based platforms and mobile applications

### 2.1.2 Improve climate services

- Develop and implement advanced climate forecasting models
- Integrate new technologies for improved climate prediction and analysis
- Expand the use of climate information services to support decision-making in various sectors, including agriculture, water resources management, and disaster risk reduction

### 2.1.3 Strengthen partnerships with stakeholders

- Establish partnerships with international organizations and research institutions to enhance capacity building and knowledge exchange
- Collaborate with regional and global climate service providers to improve the quality and reliability of climate information services
- Foster public-private partnerships to leverage private sector expertise and resources for climate services development and delivery

## 2.2 Strengthen NMHS capacities in climate risk management, adaptation planning, and disaster response

### 2.2.1 Improve climate risk assessment capabilities

- Develop and implement advanced climate risk assessment tools
- Integrate climate risk information into national and local decision-making processes
- Enhance the use of climate risk information for early warning systems and disaster preparedness planning

### 2.2.2 Enhance adaptation planning and implementation

- Support the development and implementation of national and local adaptation plans
- Provide technical assistance to NMHS and other stakeholders in integrating climate risk information into sector-specific adaptation planning
- Facilitate the sharing of best practices and lessons learned among countries and regions

### 2.2.3 Strengthen disaster response capabilities

- Develop and implement advanced early warning systems for climate-related disasters
- Enhance the capacity of NMHS to provide timely and accurate information during disasters
- Support the development of contingency plans and emergency response protocols for climate-related disasters

## 2.3 Promote regional cooperation and integration in climate services

### 2.3.1 Strengthen regional networks

- Establish and strengthen regional networks for climate services and early warning systems
- Facilitate the exchange of information, data, and best practices among NMHS in the region
- Support the development of regional climate centers to enhance cooperation and integration

### 2.3.2 Enhance regional capacity building

- Develop and implement regional training programs for NMHS staff
- Provide technical assistance to NMHS in the region to improve their capacities in climate services and early warning systems
- Facilitate the sharing of resources and expertise among NMHS in the region

### 2.3.3 Promote regional integration

- Support the development of regional policies and standards for climate services and early warning systems
- Facilitate the integration of national climate services with regional and global climate service providers
- Enhance cooperation and coordination among NMHS in the region to improve the quality and reliability of climate information services

## E

- DMI activities to be funded by GCF:
  - DMI activities include:
    - Developing and implementing the process for climate change adaptation planning in collaboration with national stakeholders, with a focus on capacity-building in climate change adaptation.
    - Establishing a climate change adaptation information system.
  - Annual reporting on the progress of the project.

## 2.2 – Strengthen climate modelling and forecasting

This activity will extend a regional Forecasting Centre to be able to provide climate services for the whole cloud region of the country, including the Pacific Ocean. The centre will be supported by developing a network of weather stations in the country, which will be used to improve the accuracy of the forecasts.

### 2.2.1 – Establish a regional Forecasting Centre

- Extend the existing forecasting centre to provide climate services for the whole cloud region.
- Develop and implement a system for multi-hazard early warning for extreme weather events (e.g., cyclones, floods).
- Strengthen the capacity of the national meteorological service to provide climate information and services.

### 2.2.2 – Establish a network of weather stations

- Establish a network of weather stations in the country.
- Develop and implement a system for multi-hazard early warning for extreme weather events (e.g., cyclones, floods).
- Strengthen the capacity of the national meteorological service to provide climate information and services.

### 2.2.3 – Strengthen capacity building

- Establish a network of weather stations in the country.
- Develop and implement a system for multi-hazard early warning for extreme weather events (e.g., cyclones, floods).
- Strengthen the capacity of the national meteorological service to provide climate information and services.

### 2.2.4 – Annual reporting on the progress of the project

- Annual reporting on the progress of the project.

### Establish climate services for health

This activity will provide:

- Monitoring, forecasting and early warning systems for health risks.
- Early warning and disease surveillance systems to improve preparedness and response to climate-related health threats. This will include:
  - Strengthening of a national health information system (HIS) with climate and environmental data.
  - Development of a climate-sensitive health risk assessment framework.
  - Establishment of an early warning system for health risks.
  - Training on the use of the system.

## E

- Establish integrated multi-hazard early warning systems and disaster risk information systems.

### 3.1 - Establish integrated multi-hazard early warning systems and disaster risk information systems
This activity will establish multi-hazard early warning systems (EWS) at the national, regional, and local levels. It aims to enhance the capacity of National Meteorological and Hydrological Services (NMHSs), Disaster Management Organizations (DMOs), and other relevant institutions to develop and implement EWS. The project will establish a network of partners to support the development of EWS in least developed countries (LDCs) and small island developing states (SIDS). It will also promote the use of innovative technologies for disaster risk reduction, including mobile applications and social media platforms.

#### 3.1.1
- Govern a technical working group for EWS implementation and operation.
- Develop and implement national and regional EWS strategies.
- Establish partnerships with international organizations to support EWS development and operation.
- Disseminate actionable information on disaster risk reduction to communities, governments, and other stakeholders.

## E

### 4.3 - Build capacity to respond to climate change and climate variability

The Activity will build the capacity of key stakeholders in the public sector, private sector, including small and medium enterprises (SMEs), and civil society organizations (CSOs) to understand, adapt to, and mitigate climate risks and vulnerabilities. This will include:

- **Enhance Stakeholder Participation:** Enhance stakeholder participation in planning and implementation of climate change adaptation activities.
- **National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA):** Strengthen the capacity of NDMA to lead and coordinate climate risk management efforts at national level.
- **Community Action Plans:** Support communities to develop community-based disaster risk management plans that integrate climate risks.
- **Integrated Water Resources Management (IWRM):** Enhance IWRM practices by strengthening water governance, improving water use efficiency, and promoting integrated approaches for water resource management.

### 4.3.1 - Enhance Stakeholder Participation

Stakeholders will be engaged in the planning and implementation of climate change adaptation activities through:

- **National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA):** Strengthen NDMA's capacity to lead and coordinate climate risk management efforts at national level.
- **Community Action Plans:** Support communities to develop community-based disaster risk management plans that integrate climate risks.

### 4.3.2 - National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA)

Strengthen the capacity of NDMA to lead and coordinate climate risk management efforts at national level through:

- **National and locally-led initiatives:** Strengthen national and local initiatives for climate change adaptation.
- **Capacity building:** Enhance the capacity of NDMA staff and other stakeholders involved in climate risk management.

### 4.3.3 - Community Action Plans

Support communities to develop community-based disaster risk management plans that integrate climate risks through:

- **Community engagement:** Engage communities in planning and implementing climate change adaptation activities.
- **Capacity building:** Build the capacity of communities to understand, adapt to, and mitigate climate risks.

### 4.3.4 - Integrated Water Resources Management (IWRM)

Enhance IWRM practices by strengthening water governance, improving water use efficiency, and promoting integrated approaches for water resource management through:

- **Water governance:** Strengthen water governance frameworks at national and local levels.
- **Water use efficiency:** Improve water use efficiency in agriculture, industry, and domestic sectors.
- **Integrated approaches:** Promote integrated approaches to water resource management that consider climate risks.

### 4.3.5 - Knowledge Sharing and Learning

Strengthen knowledge sharing and learning on climate change adaptation through:

- **National and local initiatives:** Support national and local initiatives for climate change adaptation.
- **Capacity building:** Build the capacity of stakeholders involved in climate risk management to share knowledge and best practices.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V2.1 | PAGE 08 OF 11

## The National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) is a statutory body for disaster management in India.

NDMA is responsible for laying down policies and planning concerning disaster management. It also coordinates response to natural calamities and provides support to states and districts in disaster preparedness and capacity building. NDMA has been actively involved in the formulation of the National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA) Act, 2005.

### This Act will introduce a new framework for disaster risk management in India.

The framework will be based on the principles of:

- **Prevention**: Reducing existing and new risks.
- **Preparedness**: Enhancing resilience to disasters.
- **Response**: Improving response capacity.
- **Recovery**: Building back better after disasters.

## E

### Assistance to local communities

- **Funding Mechanism**: GEF Small Grants Programme (SGP)
- **Executing Agency**: National Association of Small Farmers (ANPEAS)
- **Project Duration**: 3 years
- **Geographical Coverage**: National
- **Objective**: To support the implementation of activities that contribute to the conservation and sustainable use of natural resources, with a focus on biodiversity.
- **Specific Activities**:
  - Training in sustainable agriculture practices.
  - Establishment of community-based natural resource management committees.
  - Development of income-generating activities based on sustainable use of natural resources.

### Monitoring, reporting and evaluation arrangements

**Responsibilities**: MEA and PMU (500 words, approximately 1 page)

The Project on Monitoring and Evaluation (M&E) aims to design a performance monitoring and evaluation system that will support the implementation of the project. The M&E framework will be developed in close collaboration with the Project Management Unit (PMU), under the guidance of the Ministry of Environment and Climate Change (MECC). The M&E system will track progress towards achieving the project's objectives, including:

- **Project targets**: Annual and monthly milestones.
- **Baseline survey**: Conducted at the beginning of the project to establish a baseline for comparison with future results.
- **Indicators**: Development of indicators to measure progress against project outputs and outcomes.

The M&E framework will be aligned with the EE and technical requirements in order to ensure that it is consistent with national policies and standards. The M&E system will include:

- **Data collection tools**: Surveys, questionnaires, and other data collection methods.
- **Training**: Provision of training on how to use the M&E tools and systems.

The PMU will be responsible for collecting, analyzing, and reporting data on project performance. The PMU will also ensure that the M&E system is used effectively by all stakeholders.

### Reporting requirements

**Responsibilities**: EE and PMU (500 words, approximately 1 page)

The Project will submit semi-annual progress reports and quarterly financial statements to the GEF and the executing agency. The reporting schedule is as follows:

- **Progress report by July for activities in June**.
- **Progress report by November for activities in October**.
- **Annual performance report by April 15 of each year**.

The PMU will be responsible for preparing and submitting these reports to the GEF.

### Evaluation

**Responsibilities**: EE and PMU (500 words, approximately 1 page)

The Project will include an evaluation component to assess its effectiveness in achieving its objectives. The evaluation will be conducted by an independent evaluator selected by the EE. The evaluation will focus on:

- **Process evaluation**: Assessment of how well the project is being implemented.
- **Impact evaluation**: Assessment of the changes brought about by the project.

The PMU will be responsible for coordinating with the evaluator and ensuring that all necessary data and information are provided to support the evaluation process.

Monitoring will also be undertaken by the AE through supervision visits and self-assessments to track progress against the project's objectives. The AE will ensure that the monitoring system is implemented, including frequency of visits between AE (UNEP) and the site as well as other components in the project. An evaluation plan will be included at the start of the project implementation.

The IETT will be responsible for managing the M&E/Tax evaluation process. The IETT will ensure that the evaluation is carried out in a timely manner and that all necessary data are collected. The IETT will also be responsible for ensuring that the evaluation is conducted in accordance with the agreed terms of reference.

The Steering Committee (SC) will oversee the implementation of the M&E/Tax evaluation process and provide guidance to the IETT on its conduct. The SC will also ensure that the evaluation findings are taken into account during the remainder of the project's operational life.

At the end of the project implementation, which is a minimum of 5 years, all data will be handed over to the UNEP Tax Unit for further analysis and use. This will include an assessment of the project's overall performance and impact on the environment. The SC will also ensure that the evaluation findings are taken into account during the remainder of the project's operational life.

The code for results monitoring and performance evaluation are included in the Project budget.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.2.1 | PAGE 184 OF 116

## Description

Limited local technical expertise in national EEAs that could result in low performance or delay implementation of the Project.

### Mitigation Measures)

- **UNEP and other funding:**
  - Establishment of a Project Management Unit (PMU) to provide guidance and ensure expertise is requested from UNEP.
  - Adequate staffing for the PMU through engagement of local experts on long-term time-limited contracts by UNEP.
  - Adequate training and capacity building for national counterparts.
- **Governance structures and implementation arrangements unaffected by Technical Partners:**
  - Project risk management frameworks to ensure effective management of risks and impacts.
  - Capacity gaps of Technical Partners are addressed and adhered.

The Project will provide technical expertise, workshops, and mentoring in-country and at regional technical meetings. It will also support the development of national technical capacity through training, internships, and secondments. The Project will be implemented by a consortium of international and local organizations with strong technical expertise and experience in the field.

### Selected Risk Factor 5

| Category       | Probability | Impact |
|----------------|-------------|--------|
| Other          | Medium      | Medium |

**Description**

Sectors and communities relying on multiple organizational/behavioral change.

### Mitigation Measures)

The project will be implemented by a consortium of national and international organizations. It is a key aspect of the Project to ensure that the Project includes a range of interventions focused on improving sectoral governance, institutional capacity, and technical skills. The Project will also include a strong focus on gender equality and social inclusion. Action Plan for Gender Equality and Social Inclusion will be developed in consultation with local communities and stakeholders.

Organizational and institutional change is very difficult to achieve and will require significant effort by the Project team. The Project will need to work closely with national governments, regional organizations, and other stakeholders to ensure that the changes are sustainable and effective. The Project will also need to provide ongoing support to these institutions to ensure that they continue to implement the changes over time.

# G GC POLICY AND STANDARDS

## G.1. Ensuring compliance with standards and policies

Green Climate Fund resources (grants, technical assistance, and co-financing) are to be used in accordance with this Proposal. The range and depth of the environmental and social safeguards proposed in this Proposal will depend on the nature and scale of the intervention. The safeguard requirements for each project category are outlined below.

### G.1.1. Environmental and Social Safeguards

Projects supported by GCF resources must be designed to avoid or minimize adverse environmental and/or social impacts, and where possible, enhance beneficial ones. To this end, the following safeguards will apply:

- **Safeguard Standard 1: Biodiversity conservation, natural habitats, and ecosystems services**
  - The Project will pay close attention to impacts on biodiversity conservation, natural habitats, and ecosystem services.
  - The Project will be designed in a manner that minimizes adverse impacts on biodiversity conservation networks. A GCF-specific biodiversity assessment is required.

- **Safeguard Standard 2: Efficient use of resources**
  - The Project will pay close attention to efficient use of resources (including water, land, and energy) and the management of waste.
  - The Project will be designed in a manner that minimizes adverse impacts on efficient use of resources. A GCF-specific resource efficiency assessment is required.

- **Safeguard Standard 3: Gender equality**
  - Gender issues are to be taken into account as part of the Project's activities.

- **Safeguard Standard 4: Indigenous peoples**
  - The Project will pay close attention to impacts on indigenous peoples and their livelihoods.
  - The Project will be designed in a manner that minimizes adverse impacts on indigenous peoples. A GCF-specific indigenous peoples assessment is required.

- **Safeguard Standard 5: Indigenous peoples**
  - The Project will pay close attention to impacts on indigenous peoples and their livelihoods.
  - The Project will be designed in a manner that minimizes adverse impacts on indigenous peoples. A GCF-specific indigenous peoples assessment is required.

- **Safeguard Standard 6: Labor and working conditions**
  - The Project will pay close attention to labor standards, working conditions, and the rights of workers.
  - The Project will be designed in a manner that minimizes adverse impacts on labor standards, working conditions, and the rights of workers. A GCF-specific labor standards assessment is required.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.2 | PAGE NFPF 118

## Subfund Target 7: Protection of tangible cultural heritage

- Tangible Cultural Heritage may not be affected by Project activities.

In addition, an Environmental and Social Action Plan (ESAP) provided in Annex E.

GCF's Environmental and Social Policy states that Projects must provide a "Preventive measures mechanism" to protect tangible cultural heritage. The GCF provides guidelines as a mechanism that ensures the Government and the Project Proponent are responsible for implementing the SRM in the field. These procedures state:

- This can Subfund a Component.
- Implementation should be included in a Component.
- Implementation needs to comply with Community, Achievements, and Trafficking.
- Human Rights issues must be addressed.
- The right to Project-Affected Communities' self-determination is seen as enhancements to the SRM.

As per the funding analysis, the Project has been assessed as Category C: Projects with Activities that may have potential or negligible adverse environmental and social impacts. The proposed project will be required to prepare an Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (ESIA) or Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP).

### G. Gender Equality and Social Inclusion (GESI)

The Funding Proposal includes a Gender Assessment and Action Plan (Annex B) that has been informed by the following:

- The gender assessment was conducted in 2019.
- The assessment included a review of existing literature, an online survey, key informant interviews, focus group discussions, and consultations with relevant stakeholders.

The assessment identified the following key findings:

- Gender roles and responsibilities are influenced by cultural norms and practices.
- Women face barriers to accessing resources and decision-making processes.
- Men and women have different needs and preferences for infrastructure and services.
- There is a need for gender-responsive planning and budgeting in the water sector.

### H. Climate Change Adaptation

The project will be implemented in accordance with the following principles:

- The project will be designed to address the impacts of climate change on water resources.
- The project will include measures to improve the resilience of water infrastructure to climate change.
- The project will promote the use of climate-resilient technologies and practices.

### I. Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS)

The project will establish an ESMS to manage environmental and social risks and impacts throughout the project cycle. The ESMS will include:

- An environmental and social impact assessment (ESIA) or environmental and social management plan (ESMP).
- A gender assessment and action plan.
- A climate change adaptation plan.

### J. Stakeholder Engagement

The project will engage with stakeholders throughout the project cycle, including:

- National and local governments.
- Civil society organizations.
- Community-based organizations.
- Private sector entities.
- International organizations.

Stakeholder engagement will be conducted through consultations, workshops, and other forms of dialogue to ensure that the project is responsive to the needs and concerns of all stakeholders.

### K. Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will include a monitoring and evaluation (M&E) system to track progress towards its objectives and outcomes. The M&E system will include:

- Indicators to measure progress.
- A results framework to track achievements.
- A system for collecting, analyzing, and reporting data.
- A process for reviewing and adjusting the project based on monitoring and evaluation findings.

### L. Financial Management

The project will be implemented in accordance with the following financial management principles:

- The project will be financed through a combination of grants and loans.
- The project will have a budget that is aligned with its objectives and activities.
- The project will have a system for tracking expenditures and ensuring accountability.
- The project will comply with relevant procurement policies and procedures.

### M. Procurement

The project will follow the procurement policies and procedures of the Green Climate Fund, including:

- A competitive bidding process for all procurements over USD 500,000.
- A system for managing contracts and ensuring accountability.
- A process for monitoring and evaluating procurement performance.

### N. Risk Management

The project will include a risk management plan to identify, assess, and manage risks throughout the project cycle. The risk management plan will include:

- A risk register that identifies potential risks and their likelihood and impact.
- A system for monitoring and managing risks.
- A process for reviewing and updating the risk management plan.

### O. Safeguards

The project will comply with the safeguard policies of the Green Climate Fund, including:

- Environmental and Social Safeguards.
- Gender Equality and Social Inclusion Safeguards.
- Climate Change Adaptation Safeguards.

### P. Reporting

The project will provide regular reports to the Green Climate Fund on its progress towards achieving its objectives and outcomes. The reporting will include:

- Annual progress reports.
- Semi-annual financial reports.
- Special reports as required by the Green Climate Fund.

### Q. Audit and Compliance

The project will comply with the audit and compliance requirements of the Green Climate Fund, including:

- An annual independent audit.
- A system for managing non-compliance and ensuring accountability.
- A process for reviewing and updating the audit and compliance plan.

### R. Closure

The project will include a closure plan that outlines the steps to be taken to ensure that the project's objectives have been achieved and that the benefits of the project are sustained over time. The closure plan will include:

- A final evaluation of the project.
- A process for transferring assets and responsibilities to the relevant authorities.
- A system for monitoring and evaluating the sustainability of the project's outcomes.

### S. Annexes

The Funding Proposal includes the following annexes:

- Annex A: Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (ESIA) or Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP).
- Annex B: Gender Assessment and Action Plan.
- Annex C: Climate Change Adaptation Plan.
- Annex D: Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS) Manual.
- Annex E: Stakeholder Engagement Plan.
- Annex F: Monitoring and Evaluation Framework.
- Annex G: Financial Management Plan.
- Annex H: Procurement Plan.
- Annex I: Risk Management Plan.
- Annex J: Safeguards Policy.
- Annex K: Reporting Template.
- Annex L: Audit and Compliance Plan.
- Annex M: Closure Plan.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.2 | PAGE 18 OF 116

## Information detailed below: gender-responsive documentation and interventions relevant to the Project are provided.

- Gender mainstreaming for climate-specific indicators and targets to each Activity is ensured that gender mainstreaming is systematically monitored and evaluated throughout the term of the Project.
- The Project will be implemented by a gender focal point, with support from the Project Team Leader, as indicated in Annex 8.
- Gender mainstreaming throughout the Project is essential to ensure that gender concerns are addressed at all levels. This includes ensuring that the entire population of fringe benefits from the Project activities and that women and men have equal access to opportunities and resources.
- The Project will engage a dedicated full-time Gender Expert for technical assistance, capacity building, and monitoring and evaluation of gender mainstreaming throughout the Project.
- The Project will make sure that existing policies, including Executive Ethics, PMJ, PMI, and Technical Review Guidelines, are all made aware of relevant gender policies and protocols (including the Time-Line for Gender Mainstreaming in the UNEP and GEF).
- All staff will be required to apply by the Gender Policy principles and support gender equality and equity.
- Framework strategies, plans, and partner agreements will recognize and respect gender equality and equity as a fundamental principle of the Project.
- The UNEP and GGF Gender Policies, codes of conduct, and other relevant documents will be part of the Project’s communication strategy to ensure that all stakeholders are aware of these policies.
- All relevant information and early warning products and services will be reviewed and advised by the Project Team Leader and gender focal point to ensure that they are gender-responsive and include gender-disaggregated data.
- Regularized capacity-building efforts focused on ensuring that staff’s needs to get gender-sensitive information and empowering women with enhanced knowledge and understanding of disaster risks are included in the Project’s activities. Gender mainstreaming approaches highlighted above.
- Monitoring and evaluation are essential parts of the Project, including a review of gender equality indicators and targets to ensure that they are relevant, achievable, and measurable.

## Reference No.: (2023/05/16-08/09/2023)
### Subject:

The Government of Timor-Leste for GEF Project Proposal "Strengthening Local Waste Management and Environmental Education in Dili City"

To:
Ministry of Environment and Climate Change
Dili, Timor-Leste

We refer to the GEF Funding Proposal submitted by the Ministry of Environment and Climate Change (MoECC) of the Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste (Timor-Leste), which was endorsed by the National Authorizing Entity (NAE) on 12 July 2023. The project aims to build capacity for effective waste management in Dili City.

The Government of Timor-Leste has requested an endorsement letter from the national designated authority for submitting the GEF Funding Proposal to the GEF Council for consideration at its 64th meeting (June 2024).

We have reviewed the GEF Funding Proposal and the associated documents. Our assessment is that the project is in accordance with the GEF's environmental and social safeguards, the project activities are consistent with the country's priorities, and the proposed budget is reasonable.

Therefore, we endorse the national designated authority for submitting the GEF Funding Proposal to the GEF Council for consideration at its 64th meeting (June 2024).

We acknowledge that the views expressed in this letter will not prejudice our own positions on the GEF matters.

Yours sincerely,

## L. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### Strengths
- High impact potential: The project will contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) and the Paris Agreement targets by 60% of the population, and the government’s climate change commitments.
- Comprehensive approach that includes cross-sectoral engagement, capacity building, and effective monitoring.
- Collaborative approach that aligns with existing initiatives, going beyond the usual climate interventions to include broader development outcomes.
- Strong governance: The Timor-Leste Secretariat is well-positioned to lead this project.

### Organizational (MO) (U) (C) (F) (W) (I)
- **Organizational capacity:** UNEP has a solid track record and expertise in the field of climate change, including experience with the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).
- **Management systems:** UNEP’s Integrated Work Plan for 2018-2021 includes a comprehensive approach to climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- **Human resources:** The project will benefit from UNEP’s existing partnerships, including with the government of Timor-Leste, which has committed to supporting the project.
- **Financial capacity:** UNEP has a strong financial base and is well-positioned to manage the project’s budget.

### Synergies with existing projects (e.g., FP169)
- The project aligns with ongoing efforts to strengthen national monitoring networks and data information systems, including the Global Environment Facility (GEF) Small Grants Programme.
- The project will complement other initiatives aimed at improving governance, rule of law, and sustainable management of natural resources.

### Points of caution

- **Capacity constraints:** The Secretariat may face challenges in managing a complex project with limited resources, requiring additional support and capacity-building efforts.
- **Coordination risks:** The project will need to coordinate closely with other initiatives and stakeholders to avoid duplication of efforts and ensure effective implementation.

### Recommendations
- The Secretariat should consider alternative financing mechanisms to ensure the long-term sustainability of the project.
- Additional technical assistance may be required to support the Secretariat in managing a complex project with limited resources.
- The Secretariat should coordinate closely with other initiatives and stakeholders to avoid duplication of efforts and ensure effective implementation.

# Green Climate Fund

## IL - Summary of the Secretariat’s assessment

### 2.1 Project Background

1. **Timor-Leste** is a least developed country (LDC) and a small island developing state (SIDS). It faces significant challenges due to climate change, including sea-level rise, increased frequency and intensity of tropical cyclones, flooding, landslides, and prolonged droughts. These events exacerbate existing vulnerabilities such as food insecurity, water scarcity, and poor health outcomes. Climate-related disasters disproportionately affect women and girls, who are often responsible for securing food, water, and fuel.

2. **Climate change** is expected to have significant impacts on Timor-Leste’s economy and society. The country relies heavily on agriculture and fisheries, which are vulnerable to climate variability and extreme weather events. Climate change will also impact infrastructure and essential services, including health care facilities, education, and water supply systems. This could lead to increased poverty levels and social instability.

3. **Timor-Leste** has a high dependence on imported fossil fuels for electricity generation and transportation. The country is committed to reducing greenhouse gas emissions and transitioning to renewable energy sources. The government has set ambitious targets for increasing the share of renewable energy in the national grid by 2030.

4. **The proposed project** aims to address these challenges through a comprehensive approach that includes climate change mitigation and adaptation measures. It will support the development of renewable energy projects, improve energy efficiency, and promote sustainable land use practices. The project will also enhance the capacity of local communities to adapt to climate change and build resilience.

5. **The project** will be implemented in collaboration with relevant government agencies, international organizations, and private sector partners. It will focus on building the capacity of local communities to implement climate change adaptation measures and promote sustainable development practices.

6. **The proposed project** is aligned with Timor-Leste’s national development priorities and contributes to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). It supports the country’s commitment to the Paris Agreement and the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

7. **The project** will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial focus on pilot projects in selected communities. The project will also include capacity building activities for government agencies and local communities.

8. **The proposed project** is expected to contribute to the achievement of Timor-Leste’s climate change targets and promote sustainable development. It will support the country’s efforts to build resilience to climate change and reduce greenhouse gas emissions.

Component II aims to develop a financial model for ICRC-NRL. The model will include an analysis of the costs associated with the development of user-friendly products and the potential revenue streams.

Component III aims to develop a financial model for SMSIA. The model will include an analysis of the costs associated with the development of user-friendly products and the potential revenue streams.

This component is the core of the project, which TPIA of the funding bodies agreed on. It includes the development of a financial model for ICRC-NRL, IFRC-NRL, and SMSIA. This component is on the one hand, the development of a financial model for ICRC-NRL, and on the other hand, the development of a financial model for SMSIA. The component also includes the development of a financial model for IFRC-NRL.

# Green Climate Fund

## Activity 1: Strengthening National Designated Authorities (NDAs)

### Objective
Strengthen the capacity of NDAs to effectively manage and implement climate change-related projects.

### Description
- Provide training on project preparation and management.
- Enhance understanding of climate change policies, strategies, and best practices.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources.

## Activity 2: Enhancing National Climate Change Planning Processes

### Objective
Support countries in developing and implementing comprehensive national climate change adaptation plans (NAPs).

### Description
- Conduct workshops and training sessions on NAP development.
- Provide guidance on integrating climate risk management into sectoral planning processes.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources.

## Component 1: Building Resilience

### Objective
Enhance the resilience of communities and ecosystems to climate change impacts.

### Description
- Conduct vulnerability assessments.
- Develop and implement early warning systems.
- Promote ecosystem-based adaptation strategies.

## Component 2: Limiting or Mitigating Greenhouse Gas Emissions (LMI)

### Objective
Reduce greenhouse gas emissions through improved land use, energy efficiency, and renewable energy projects.

### Description
- Support the development of climate-smart agriculture practices.
- Promote energy efficiency in buildings and industries.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for renewable energy projects.

## Component 3: Knowledge Management

### Objective
Strengthen knowledge management systems to support decision-making and learning across sectors.

### Description
- Develop and maintain a climate change information portal.
- Conduct regular workshops and training sessions on climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for knowledge management activities.

## Component 4: Capacity Building

### Objective
Enhance the capacity of national and local institutions to effectively manage climate change projects.

### Description
- Provide training on project preparation, implementation, and monitoring.
- Develop and implement a certification program for climate change professionals.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for capacity building activities.

## Component 5: Cross-Sectoral Coordination

### Objective
Improve coordination among government agencies, civil society organizations, and the private sector to enhance climate change responses.

### Description
- Conduct regular workshops and training sessions on cross-sectoral collaboration.
- Develop and implement a communication strategy for climate change initiatives.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for cross-sectoral coordination activities.

## Component 6: Monitoring, Evaluation, and Reporting (MER)

### Objective
Strengthen the capacity of NDAs to monitor, evaluate, and report on climate change projects.

### Description
- Develop and implement a MER framework.
- Conduct regular workshops and training sessions on MER methodologies.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for MER activities.

## Component 7: Knowledge Sharing

### Objective
Facilitate the sharing of knowledge, best practices, and lessons learned among countries and stakeholders.

### Description
- Develop and maintain a climate change information portal.
- Conduct regular workshops and training sessions on knowledge sharing strategies.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for knowledge sharing activities.

## Component 8: Community Engagement

### Description
- Conduct community consultations and workshops.
- Develop and implement community-based adaptation projects.
- Facilitate access to technical assistance and financial resources for community engagement activities.

# Baseline evaluation programs

Baseline evaluation programs, at each monitoring or area vulnerability and capacity assessment and other "baseline" local-level analysis will feed into the development of national-level indicators to monitor progress towards achieving the objectives of the National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan (NBSAP). The capacity of the National Biodiversity Management Directorate, responsible for coordinating biodiversity-related activities at the national level, will be built. The general public will also be observed through baseline assessments and other monitoring programs.

# The proposal adequately articulates the impacts and benefits in terms of (i) health, (ii) social inclusion, (iii) biodiversity, (iv) supporting public and environmental health, (v) enhancing community resilience, (vi) promoting sustainable livelihoods, and (vii) contributing to climate change mitigation. The proposal has a high potential to enhance impacts on the ground.

## 3.2 Paradigm shift

### i.
The proposal is sufficiently articulated the challenges, opportunities and the need for transformational change in the way that development is conceptualized and implemented.

### ii.
Transformational planning and programming. The proposal aims to optimize the use of resources through a paradigm shift towards transformational planning and programming. This includes gap filling and addressing the capacity constraints of existing institutions. It also involves strengthening the enabling environment, including institutional arrangements, in order to enhance information and knowledge sharing (WMO; ITC; UNDP; WHO). The proposal has identified key stakeholders and methodologies as creating evidence, and developing indicators to measure progress towards transformational change. This includes the following: (a) Transformational planning model and guidance for climate information and health-related actions.

### iii.
Catalysing climate information. The proposal has adopted some of the best available practices in order to enhance the use of climate information in decision-making processes. It also aims to strengthen the capacity of stakeholders to use climate information, including through the development of a Climate Information Services (CIS) framework. This includes the following: (a) Developing and implementing a CIS framework that is tailored to the needs of different users; (b) Enhancing the capacity of stakeholders to use climate information in decision-making processes.

### iv.
The proposal builds on existing initiatives, including the WHO/UNICEF/UNDP/World Bank Group Joint Action for Climate and Health Equity (JA4CHE), which aims to strengthen health systems' resilience to climate change. The proposal also builds on the work of the World Health Organization's (WHO) Health and Climate Change Office, which provides technical support to countries in integrating climate change into health policies and programs.

### v.
The proposal is based on existing initiatives, including the WHO/UNICEF/UNDP/World Bank Group Joint Action for Climate and Health Equity (JA4CHE), which aims to strengthen health systems' resilience to climate change. The proposal also builds on the work of the World Health Organization's (WHO) Health and Climate Change Office, which provides technical support to countries in integrating climate change into health policies and programs.

### vi.
In addition, it will promote additional linkages between sectors of the priority sectors for health, environment and development. # Green Climate Fund

## Needs of the recipient

### Scale N/A

This proposal is built on the country programme which has been prepared by the Government of Tuvalu. The Government of Tuvalu has identified the need to address climate change and its impacts as a priority area for development assistance. This was further confirmed during the GCF country visit in 2018 where extensive consultations were undertaken with key stakeholders including government, private sector, civil society and communities.

The Government of Tuvalu is committed to addressing climate change through a comprehensive approach that includes mitigation and adaptation actions. The Government has identified priority areas for action which include: (i) enhancing the resilience of infrastructure and communities to climate impacts; (ii) improving water security and food security; (iii) promoting sustainable economic growth; and (iv) strengthening disaster risk management.

The proposed project will support the implementation of these priorities through a comprehensive approach that includes both mitigation and adaptation actions. The project will also support the development of a national climate change policy framework, which is currently under development. This will include the development of a national greenhouse gas inventory, a national climate change adaptation strategy, and a national climate change mitigation strategy.

## Country ownership

### Scale N/A

The proposal has been identified as a country priority expressed to the Green Climate Fund (GCF) through the Government of Tuvalu's Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs). The Government of Tuvalu has committed to reduce its greenhouse gas emissions by 30% below business-as-usual levels by 2030. This commitment is in line with the Government's broader development objectives and is a key component of the country's climate change policy framework.

The proposed project will be implemented through a comprehensive approach that includes both mitigation and adaptation actions. The project will also support the development of a national climate change policy framework, which is currently under development. This will include the development of a national greenhouse gas inventory, a national climate change adaptation strategy, and a national climate change mitigation strategy.

# Green Climate Fund

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) aims to support and promote a paradigm shift towards low-emission and climate-resilient development pathways by providing support to developing countries to enable them to limit or reduce their greenhouse gas emissions and adapt to the impacts of climate change.

### Objectives

- **Mitigation**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Adaptation**: Enhance adaptive capacity, resilience, and effective response to climate change in developing countries.

## GCF Structure

The Green Climate Fund is governed by a Board composed of 24 members from developed and developing countries. The Board provides strategic direction and makes decisions on funding proposals. The Secretariat, based in Songdo, South Korea, implements the Board's decisions and manages the Fund's resources.

### Key Functions

- **Funding**: Approves funding for projects and programs.
- **Accreditation**: Evaluates and accredits entities that can access GCF funds.
- **Monitoring and Evaluation**: Ensures effective use of funds and tracks progress towards climate goals.

## GCF Activities

GCF supports a wide range of activities, including:

- **Renewable Energy**: Promoting solar, wind, and other renewable energy sources.
- **Energy Efficiency**: Improving energy efficiency in buildings and industries.
- **Climate-Smart Agriculture**: Enhancing agricultural practices to adapt to climate change.
- **Disaster Risk Reduction**: Building resilience to natural disasters.

### Funding Approaches

GCF provides funding through various approaches:

- **Mitigation**: Projects that reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Adaptation**: Projects that enhance resilience to climate impacts.
- **Cross-Cutting**: Projects that address both mitigation and adaptation.

## Risks

### Overall Project Assessment (Medium Risk)

GCF is expected to provide a grant of USD 20 million to France for climate information systems. The project aims to improve the availability, accessibility, and use of climate information services in Africa. The overall risk assessment is medium due to potential challenges in implementation and coordination.

#### Timeframe

- **LCF**: Long-term financing.
- **IDA**: International Development Association financing.

### Specific Risks

- **Implementation Risk**: Medium due to the complexity of the project and the need for effective coordination among various stakeholders.
- **Financial Risk**: Medium, given the significant funding involved and the need for careful financial management.

## Conclusion

GCF plays a crucial role in supporting developing countries in their efforts to mitigate climate change and adapt to its impacts. The Fund's activities are wide-ranging and include support for renewable energy, energy efficiency, climate-smart agriculture, and disaster risk reduction. Despite some risks, the overall assessment of the project is medium, indicating a balanced approach to addressing potential challenges.

# Financial rules of GNEP

Financial rules of GNEP, which follow International Public Sector Accounting Standards (IPSAIs), are promulgated pursuant to the Financial Regulations and Rules of the United Nations. While the financial rules of GNEP are designed to ensure compliance with IPSAIs, they also address specific needs and requirements, including eligibility criteria, temporal exclusion provisions, quality assurance, and other matters relevant to the United Nations. The rules have been developed in close consultation with the General Assembly’s Fifth Committee, which oversees the budget and financial management of the Organization. During the organization’s inception phase, GNEP will conduct more detailed assessments of these rules and their application to the specific needs of the Organization. This assessment will enable the programme to ensure that such rules can best be applied in a manner consistent with the principles of transparency, accountability, and integrity.

# Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment of FP171

**Proposed theme:** Enhancing social safeguards to hold private enterprises to account for socio-environmental impacts in Timor-Leste.

**Accredited entity:** United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP).

**Country(ies):** Democratic Republic of Timor-Leste.

**Proposed/preparer name:** Small.

## I. Assessment of the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

### 1.1 Impact potential
**Scale: High**

Timor-Leste is a poor, coastal, family-dependent country in the Asia-Pacific region. This project aims to enhance social safeguards and accountability mechanisms for private enterprises operating in the extractive industries sector. The project will support the government of Timor-Leste to develop and implement a social safeguard system that ensures that private enterprises respect human rights and environmental standards.

The project will also provide technical assistance to the government to strengthen its capacity to monitor and enforce these standards. This will help to ensure that private enterprises are held accountable for their actions and that they comply with international standards. The project will also support the development of a national social safeguard system that is consistent with international best practices.

## In view of the independent TAP arrangements with global regulatory controls it is considered appropriate to include the TAP in the list of bodies, institutions and organizations that are relevant to the implementation of EPL. A further community analysis revealed a combination of increasing costs and declining benefit streams is an impediment to per capita recruitment to the NPV of $150.81 initially estimated for the project. The analysis also indicates that the project has not yet reached its full potential in terms of economic benefits, and that the current level of participation is not sufficient to achieve the desired outcomes.

## b) The Board's recommendation

The Board recommends that the project be continued and that the current level of participation be increased to ensure that the project reaches its full potential. The Board also recommends that the project be monitored on an ongoing basis to ensure that it continues to meet the objectives set out in the business plan.

## c) The funding proposal

The funding proposal is optimistic in terms of cost modelling, diversity and the volume of data collected. The Board recommends that the project be funded for a period of three years with a view to demonstrating the feasibility of the approach. The Board also recommends that the project be monitored on an ongoing basis to ensure that it continues to meet the objectives set out in the business plan.

## d) The demonstration project

The demonstration project is intended to demonstrate the feasibility of the approach and to provide information on the potential benefits of the approach. The Board recommends that the project be funded for a period of three years with a view to demonstrating the feasibility of the approach. The Board also recommends that the project be monitored on an ongoing basis to ensure that it continues to meet the objectives set out in the business plan.

## II. Overall remarks from the independent Technical Advisory Panel

On the basis of the above findings, the independent TAP recommends that the Board approve the project.
